# Running

Current prepared-service result (2026-09-13): the full owned-output service passes all five gates on RTX 5070, including every warm L query <=2 ms. Modal L4 passes numerical/lifecycle gates but fails latency. See the prepared local interval results below for setup costs, complete captures and retained failures.

Status values: VERIFIED-FRESH = the module's own gates passed in the recorded run; OWN-GATE-FAIL = one or more own gates failed, result retained and unpromoted; CUDA-ONLY = GPU execution not yet verified in the recorded scope; SYNTHETIC-ONLY = passing evidence limited to synthetic inputs. Earlier attempts are notes, not additional current module rows.

1. `python -m venv .venv && .venv/bin/pip install -r requirements.txt`.
2. Engine code lives under `src/field_engine/`; standalone diagnostics live under `probes/field_engine/`. Run the documented entrypoint for each row and inspect its reported gates and evidence paths. Some legacy self-tests exit zero even when an internal gate is false.
3. `pytest tests/` wraps those self-tests plus the recipe/harness/surrogate and closed-loop tests; the latest complete CPU run took 317 seconds (71 passed, five GPU-dependent skips, four Gaussian-process fit warnings; `reports/final_2200_pytest.json`).
4. Warp uses a CUDA device when one is present and falls back to its CPU backend otherwise. Most results here were produced on the CPU backend, where the float32-atomic determinism case cannot fail by construction. The benchmark rows that need a device (marked `RTX 5070` in `docs/BENCHMARKS.md`) were measured on an RTX 5070 (Warp 1.17.0, CUDA 12.9) on 2026-09-12, and there the float32-atomic reduction over three processes does fail as predicted: all six values over two runs differ, spread 1.9e-6 to 7.1e-6 relative on a ~2.6e6 sum, while the int64 fixed-point reduction stays bit-identical. That is the reason the shipped reduction is the fixed-point one.
5. The inputs are synthetic and generated in code (a turned profile for the manufacturing field, a bolted plate and load case for the load field, analytic bodies for the kernel and renderer tests), so nothing outside this repository is needed.
6. The factory input for `fabriksnegativrum_v1.py` lives in `examples/factory_synth/` and is regenerated by `python examples/factory_synth/gen_factory_synth_v1.py`; point the module at another directory with `FIELD_ENGINE_FACTORY_DATA`. Run the hall itself with `python src/field_engine/fabriksnegativrum_v1.py --kor` (about 30 s on a CPU).
7. `faltkarna_v1_svep_v2_loft.py` runs four gates and all four pass on a fresh run here. Two references and one tolerance were wrong and are corrected. The loft builds every cross-section as the n-gon inscribed in the circle (`build_ring_polygon`), so gate A now compares against that n-gon's volume, `ngon_area_factor(28)` = 3.11529 per r^2, a factor 0.99163 of pi, i.e. 59813.6 mm3 instead of 60318.6 mm3 for the 300 mm pipe; 0.84 % of the 4.51 % it used to report was the polygon discretisation of its own reference. Its 3 % tolerance was resolution-blind: an occupancy is a set of whole voxels, so each of the two lateral faces is resolved to at worst half a voxel and the volume error is bounded by (pitch/2) * A_surface / V, which for a 2 mm wall at pitch 0.5 mm -- four voxels across the wall -- is 25.2 % (A_surface = 60192 mm2, the inscribed n-gon's inner plus outer perimeter times the length). The tolerance is now that bound, computed from the actual pitch (`rasterisation_tolerance`), and the gate additionally requires the measurement to stay inside the bound of every pitch in {0.5, 0.35, 0.25} mm, where the bound tightens to 25.2 / 17.6 / 12.6 %: measured 3.70 / 0.01 / 0.57 %, all green. The 3.70 % at 0.5 mm is grid phase, not sweep error -- the wall radii 15 and 17 mm are exact multiples of that pitch and fall on the grid planes, and moving the window padding 6.0 -> 6.125 mm, which shifts the grid a quarter voxel and nothing else, gives 0.69 %. Gate C compared the swept occupancy with the naive pipe formula 2*pi*R*pi*r^2, which is Pappus' theorem and therefore subtracts the part of the section lying beyond the axis - precisely the doubly covered core that the even-odd fill of the self-intersecting mesh also drops, so the two errors cancelled and both tori read 0.86 % / 0.89 % off. The reference is now the volume actually swept by the n-gon section, `ngon_sweep_reference_volume`, which equals the naive formula when the rings do not overlap: the R > r torus reads 58709.2 mm3 against 58721.9 mm3, 0.02 %, and the R < r torus 9781.5 mm3 against 10560.3 mm3, 7.37 %, so the detector separates by a factor 340 at C's unchanged 3 % tolerance. Two other candidate signals were measured and rejected: the ratio against the naive formula (0.9914 vs 0.9911) and a ray along the loop axis, which finds the through-hole empty in both cases (0.0 vs 0.0) because the fill drops the core the R < r case should have there. The gate run takes about 25 s.

8. The op-schema chain lives in `src/field_engine/recipe/`: `cad_op_schema_v1.py` validates a recipe's op dicts, `cad_op_exec_v1.py` executes them against build123d, `geometri_selektor_v1.py` addresses faces and edges geometrically instead of by kernel index, `recept_cache_v1.py` caches op prefixes by chain hash, and `formfeature_v1.py` and `brep_recept_synth_v1.py` build on top of them. Every one of them is a script with its own selftest (`python src/field_engine/recipe/<module>.py`, plus `selftest` for the selector module and `--selftest` for the recipe synthesiser), and each writes its report and cache under `src/field_engine/recipe/artifacts/`, which is git-ignored.
9. All 20 op types with a handler execute here. `formrib_v1.py` (the rib form verb) and `sketch_gcs_v1.py` (the parametric sketch and its constraint solver) ship in `src/field_engine/recipe/`, so the two handlers that import them lazily, `rib` and `sketch_profile`, resolve. The executor's selftest covers both on the synthetic bracket plate (AT2h: a rib welded on the plate's top face, measured 154080.0 mm3 against the closed-form 154080.0 mm3; AT2i: the plate outline solved as a constraint graph, status FULLT_BESTAMD, extruded to 151200.0 mm3 against the analytic 151200.0 mm3), and AT2j asserts that every op type the schema declares has a handler and that both lazy imports resolve. `reference` is the one op type the schema declares and the executor deliberately refuses with NotImplementedError (21 declared types, 20 handlers).
10. `brep_recept_synth_v1.py` round-trips five selftest parts. Exact (0.0 % volume deviation, identical face count and face mix): the plate with four through holes, 148787.256842043 mm3 against 148787.256842043 mm3, 10 faces vs 10, {PLANE 6, CYLINDER 4}; the same plate with a 2 mm chamfer on its four straight top edges, 147877.92350870953 mm3 against 147877.92350870965 mm3, 14 vs 14, {PLANE 10, CYLINDER 4}, rebuilt from four emitted `chamfer` ops at a measured distance of 2.0 mm each; and the same plate with a 3 mm fillet on one straight top edge, 148613.42935439534 mm3 against 148613.4293543949 mm3, 11 vs 11, {PLANE 6, CYLINDER 5}, rebuilt from one emitted `fillet` op at the measured radius 3.0 mm, addressed by the measured midpoint (70, 0, 6) of the edge it rounded. Not exact, for a declared reason: an UNEQUAL-leg chamfer. The recogniser measures both legs of a 2 mm x 4 mm band (4.0 and 2.0, bisector deviation 18.43 degrees), but `OP_SPECS["chamfer"]` carries a single scalar (`distance` XOR `radius_frac`) and the schema is frozen, so no op is emitted and the rebuilt solid keeps the material that band removed: the plate with one asymmetric chamfer rebuilds 360.0000000002 mm3 high, 0.242543 %, with 10 faces against 11 (one PLANE missing); a plate with two through holes, one 2 mm chamfer, one 2 x 4 chamfer and one 3 mm fillet on three edges that share no corner rebuilds 360.0000000000 mm3 high, 0.241158 %, 10 faces against 11, with its chamfer, fillet and both holes rebuilt exactly. The excess in both cases is the closed-form volume of the un-cut wedge (0.5 x 2 x 4 x 90 = 360.0 mm3) to within 2e-10 mm3, so the whole deviation is that one band and nothing else, and that is what the selftest gates on.

11. Small features in the mesh-to-SDF path have their own measurement, `mesh_to_sdf_small_features_v1.py --small-features` (about 6 s on a CPU). It rasterises the plate from `examples/parts/holed_plate_v1.py` (through holes of radius 1, 2, 4, 8 and 16 mm at one thickness; run that file to regenerate its STL) at pitches 2, 1, 0.5 and 0.25 mm and measures per hole the radial deviation of the zero level set, whether the wall is still one closed ring, and the hole volume against the closed form. At a fixed pitch the deviation is the pitch divided by the hole radius, so it is the ratio R/h alone that decides: 1 mm hole at 2 mm pitch 201 %, 16 mm hole at the same pitch 12.5 %, and the hole volume error follows the same diagonal (-12.5 % at two voxels per radius, -0.1 % at 64). The ring test never actually broke on this part where it is measurable: down to two voxels per radius the wall is still one closed ring, and at one voxel per radius or less the test itself has no room (the circle at R - h has non-positive radius) and the 1 mm hole comes out 27 % too large instead. The scalloped look therefore comes from a level set that is half a voxel to a voxel off all round, not from a wall that has fallen apart. Two changes address it, both in `faltkarna_v1_mesh_to_sdf.py`: `ytkorrektion` (on by default, `FALTKARNA_SDF_YTKORREKTION=0` to disable) subtracts the half pitch by which a distance transform of a voxel mask overstates the distance to the surface, which halves the deviation and leaves the delivered occupancy bit-identical; and `feature_radius_min` (off by default, `"auto"` to measure it from the mesh) picks the pitch from the smallest radius of curvature in the mesh as h <= R_min / 6. With both on, the plate rasterises at h = 0.167 mm and every hole of radius 2 mm and up lands within 4.0 % (R = 2) / 2.1 % (R = 4) / 1.0 % (R = 8) / 0.5 % (R = 16) with one ring and a volume error of at most 3.0 %; the 1 mm hole reads 8.1 %, one ring, -1.9 % volume. `feature_radius_min` stays off by default because the other caller of `mesh_to_sdf_del`, `faltkarna_v1_svep_v2_loft.py`, is CUDA-only here and its numbers was re-measured on CUDA on 2026-09-12 (see note 7: all four gates green).

12. The benchmark family lives in `src/field_engine/bench/` and is documented with its numbers in `docs/BENCHMARKS.md`. One driver (`faltbench_v1.py`) runs each component (`_komp_*.py`) in its own subprocess under `nice -n 15` with at most four CPU threads, aggregates the part files under `src/field_engine/bench/artifacts/_parts/`, appends one row to `faltbench_trend_v1.jsonl` and applies a gate with pre-registered thresholds (`THRESH`) against a baseline that only `--rebaseline --reason "..."` writes. `pytest tests/test_bench.py` runs every component in its reduced sizes; the rows that need a CUDA device (size L of the field kernel, of the mesh-to-SDF op and of the expression tree, and the two device-against-CPU rows) are skipped without one. They were measured on an RTX 5070 on 2026-09-12 and are marked `RTX 5070` in `docs/BENCHMARKS.md`; with a device present `pytest -k bench` is 10 passed instead of 7 passed and 3 skipped. Every benchmark part is generated by this repository: the analytic test field and sphere, closed analytic bodies, the holed plate and the recipe bracket.

13. The duct line lives in `src/field_engine/lbm_domare_v1.py` (the lattice-Boltzmann pressure-drop judge) and `src/field_engine/opt/duct_growth_diff_v1.py` / `f33_1_topologi_v1.py` (radius descent and route-topology proposal). All three run on the CPU, one stage per invocation: `selftest`, then `calib` / `plenum` / `kcands` / `report` for the judge, `descend` / `judge_before` / `judge_after` / `report` for the descent, and `generate` first for the topology module. The judge must be run before the other two: they read its bent-duct measurement to calibrate the bend-loss coefficient (`artifacts/lbm_domare_v1.json`, or the `calib` partial). All geometry is declared in `lbm_domare_v1.py`: `DUCT_CROSS_MM` is the duct cross-section and `SYNTH_PLENUM_MM` a rectangular chamber with one inlet and one outlet, sized so every task the corpus generator writes fits inside it. The duct corpus is regenerated with

        python src/field_engine/duct_task_gen_v1.py --n 11 --seed 20260730 --out-dir data/corpus/duct_v1

    and is committed (140 kB); point the two `opt/` modules elsewhere with `FIELD_ENGINE_DUCT_CORPUS`. The k-candidate sweep is 12 separate short runs, `python src/field_engine/lbm_domare_v1.py kcand1 <leg_mm> <coarse|fine>` for each of 60/75/90/105/120/135 mm, folded by the `kcands` stage.

14. The drawing generator lives in `src/field_engine/drawing/` and needs FreeCAD 1.1, which is an
    OPTIONAL dependency and is not installed by `requirements.txt`. Obtain FreeCAD 1.1 (the AppImage
    from the FreeCAD release page, or a distribution/conda package that ships the `TechDraw` module)
    and point the environment variable `FREECAD_CMD` at the executable, for example
    `FREECAD_CMD=/path/to/FreeCAD-1.1.AppImage`; the CLI argument `--freecad-cmd` overrides it. There
    is no default path. `ritning_gen_v1.py` starts that executable in console mode and runs
    `ritning_gen_v1_fcworker.py` inside it: the worker reads the STEP files, builds one
    `TechDraw::DrawViewPart` per view, exports each view's hidden-line projection as SVG, measures the
    tight bounding box and the inner cylinders, and calibrates the 3D-to-sheet frame by matching its
    own projection against the view's own visible vertices (a view whose frame is contradicted gets no
    dimensions). The sheet, title block, dimensions and parts list are composed by the driver on top of
    FreeCAD's own ISO 5457 templates, which are copied out of the FreeCAD installation on first use
    into `src/field_engine/drawing/artifacts/_mallar/` (this repository carries no copy of them).
    `ritning_paritet_v1.py` is the gate and shares no code with the generator: it re-reads the STEP
    through OCP in its own process and compares against the numbers parsed out of the SVG, over three
    channels (geometry parity, drawing self-consistency, title-block mass) plus a line-work floor, and
    it re-runs itself against a copy of the sheet with one dimension shifted by 1 mm, which must fail.
    `python src/field_engine/drawing/ritning_gen_v1.py selftest` runs the calibration proof, exports
    the synthetic bracket (`examples/parts/bracket_step_v1.py`, the repository's own recipe part at a
    declared theta, 134309.0266 mm3, 0.363 kg) and drives both. Measured here with FreeCAD 1.1.1: the
    calibration reference sign convention wins unambiguously on both selftest views (hit fraction
    0.889 against 0.444/0.0/0.0 on the front view, 0.556 against 0.444 on the top view), the sheet
    comes out A3 at 1:1 with the isometric at 1:2, 100 path elements and 7 dimensions, and the gate is
    green 7/7: the three overall dimensions read 150 / 100 / 10 mm against OCP's optimal bounding box
    with a deviation of 0.0 mm each, the hole callout reads 4 x Ø10 against 4 holes of that diameter
    whose axis is parallel to the top view's direction, the title-block mass 0.363 kg equals the mass
    re-summed from the manifest, and the falsification run shifts one dimension by 1 mm, measures
    exactly 1.0 mm of injected deviation and fails as it must. PDF export is attempted through
    playwright and is skipped when it is absent.
15. The topology instrument `src/field_engine/ikarus_v1/genus_v1.py` measures a mesh's boundary
    topology per connected component. Its point is that a hand-rolled per-component Euler count and
    `manifold3d.genus()` answer different questions and are linked by the identity
    `m3d_genus == handles_summa - (n_components - 1)`, so `m3d_genus` alone cancels a tunnel against
    an enclosed void and must never be a gate on its own. `python genus_v1.py --selftest` measures
    both instruments on the same triangulation for seven constructed bodies: 7/7 pass, the identity
    holds 7/7, and exactly 1 collision pair is found (the block with one through hole plus one
    enclosed cavity reads the same `m3d_genus = 0` as the flawless block, while handles/voids separate
    them 1/1 against 0/0). `manifold3d` is in `requirements.txt` for that comparison.
16. The Lipschitz-spine verification cells run on a synthetic room generated in code.
    `synthetic_room_v1.py` declares a rectangular room (interior 200 x 150 x 100 mm, 8 mm walls) with
    a partition at x = 100 mm built from two internal wall segments that leave a 20 mm doorway, takes
    a Euclidean distance transform of the occupancy at 1 mm pitch for the clearance field, and lays a
    54-station floor route at z = 30 mm straight through the doorway. `lipschitz_spine_v1_verify.py`
    builds a hollow duct on it through `hollow_duct_from_spine` with a deliberately ambitious 12 mm
    desired radius, and `lipschitz_spine_v1_locate_cavities.py` isolates any boundary component that
    is not the main shell. Both were run here on the Warp CPU backend
    (`FIELD_ENGINE_DEVICE=cpu`, about 35 s and 3 s). Measured: the primitive shrinks 9 of 54 stations,
    `r_used` = [7.50, 12.00] mm, and that matches the room's independently smoothed reference profile
    to a mean 0.0936 mm and a maximum 1.3764 mm against the derived tolerance 4.19 mm; the duct
    measures `handles_summa` = 1, `n_hallrum` = 0, watertight, one boundary component, identical at
    0.8 mm and 0.45 mm marching cubes (59920 / 190641 vertices), so the convergence gate holds; the
    blind-lumen discriminant gives handles 0 and one enclosed void as it must; an extreme 60 mm
    request shrinks all 54 stations with a margin slack of 0.0 mm and drops none; the tree round-trips
    through `expr.save`/`expr.load` identically. VERDICT PASS. The cavity locator finds 0 cavities and
    its labelling agrees with `genus_v1`. Note that the two residual sub-millimetre voids
    `lipschitz_spine_v1.py`'s own docstring warns about do NOT appear on this room and mouth geometry;
    that caution is about wide mouths and is not refuted by this narrower case.

17. Per-block pitch. `faltkarna_v1_multires.py --multires` (about 40 s with a CUDA device) builds a
    two-level block-sparse field in which every active block carries its own pitch out of
    {h, h/2, h/4} and measures it against the two single-pitch builds of note 11 on the same plate.
    The level of a block comes from `minsta_feature_radie` evaluated on the submesh of triangles
    that touch that block: the coarsest level with h_l <= r_local / 10. The factor is 10 rather than
    the global path's 6 because a per-block pitch has no slack: the deviation a hole of radius R
    reads at its own pitch is h_l/(2R), so the 5 % gate is h_l <= R/10 at that same R, while a global
    pitch chosen from the smallest feature is already far finer than R/10 at every larger one.
    Measured on `examples/parts/holed_plate_v1.py` with h = 0.667 mm (so h/4 = 0.167 mm, note 11's
    pitch): radial deviation of the zero level set / one ring / hole volume error, multires vs
    global-fine vs global-coarse, is 8.2 / 4.0 / 4.1 / 4.1 / 2.1 % against 8.2 / 4.0 / 2.1 / 1.0 /
    0.5 % and 33.2 / 16.5 / 8.3 / 4.1 / 2.1 % at R = 1, 2, 4, 8, 16 mm, every cell one closed ring,
    volume error at most 3.0 % (multires), 2.9 % (fine), 5.1 % (coarse). The multires structure holds
    2 039 808 active voxels against global-fine's 10 420 224, a ratio of 0.196 at 526 active blocks
    (416 at h, 62 at h/2, 48 at h/4), and builds in 1.6 s against 3.4 s for global-fine and 0.25 s
    for global-coarse. Continuity across a pitch change is enforced, not hoped for: the fine block's
    face layer is overwritten by the coarse neighbour's field interpolated in the coarse block's own
    face plane, which reproduces that bilinear function exactly on the refined face lattice. Measured
    over all 96 block faces where the pitch changes, 59 744 sample points, the max jump of the
    delivered query is 0.000500 mm against the declared bound (coarser pitch / 2) 0.3333 mm; with the
    band switched off the same measurement reads 0.666572 mm, i.e. exactly one coarse pitch, and is
    red. Two builds of the whole structure are bit-identical (sha256 over block kinds, levels and all
    tiles). The gradient query reads a median |grad sd| of 0.9985 on the 16 mm hole wall. The gate
    (fine-table accuracy for R >= 2 mm at <= 30 % of global-fine's active voxels) passes on the plate.
    It does not pass on the synthetic bracket, and that is the honest limit of the method: there the
    smallest curvature radius is 4.49 mm, so h = 2.99 mm, a block is 23.9 mm across, and 70 of the 80
    active blocks (none at level 0) end up at h/4 -- the refinement cannot localise inside blocks that
    large, and the structure holds 2 334 720 active voxels against global-fine's 718 848, a ratio of
    3.25 while matching its accuracy (median |sd| at 4000 triangle centroids 0.1381 mm against
    0.1379 mm, coarse 0.7165 mm). Per-block pitch pays when the pitch is forced by a feature much
    smaller than the part and the blocks are small against the features; it costs when almost every
    surface block has to be refined anyway.

### Field to recipe, first step

`field_to_recipe_v1.py --selftest` closes the reverse direction on the two parts whose recipe is
known independently: the synthetic bracket (140 x 90 x 12 plate, four 8 mm through holes) and the
holed plate (five through holes, radii 1 to 16 mm). Each part is rasterised to a block-sparse SDF by
the shipped rasteriser (bracket pitch 1.0 mm, 684 active blocks of 684; plate pitch 0.5 mm, 1320 of
1344), the zero level set is meshed (63 772 and 113 128 triangles), planes and cylinders are fitted
to it, and the fitted B-rep goes into the existing recogniser and synthesiser. Both emitted recipes
are `sketch_2d` + `extrude` + one `hole` per bore, and both execute: bracket volume deviation
0.1069 % against that pitch's rasterisation bound 10.59 %, face mix 6 PLANE + 4 CYLINDER in and out;
plate 0.1263 % against bound 10.91 %, face mix 6 PLANE + 5 CYLINDER in and out. The fitted radii are
3.866 mm (true 4.0) and 0.934 / 1.933 / 3.942 / 7.959 / 15.981 mm (true 1 / 2 / 4 / 8 / 16), i.e.
within 0.13 and 0.07 of a pitch -- the voxel field's own resolution, which is why the gate is the
rasterisation bound and not a fixed percentage. Primitive coverage of those two surfaces is 96.29 %
and 95.41 %; the remainder is the rounding the field puts on the outer edges and the bore rims.

On a topology-optimised field (`lastfalt_v1_topopt` on its own synthetic case, 32 x 32 x 5, volume
fraction 0.35, final compliance 29 257.5 Nmm) the same fit covers **67.05 %** of the boundary area
(323 238 mm2 of 482 084 mm2) with 3 planes and 1 cylinder, within 3.6 mm, which is 0.75 of that
field's own 4.8 mm pitch. The remaining 158 846 mm2 is free-form and **no recipe is claimed for it**:
the module reports the coverage fraction, nothing more. Two runs of the selftest write byte-identical
stdout (no random numbers, every ordering taken from a measured quantity).

## Status

Engine modules only. Each row states its own result or baseline scope; diagnostic
measurements and verification commands have separate tables below.

**S1** is the complete owned-output service latency gate: every warm L query
must finish within 2 ms, including copies. This criterion applies only to rows
that name S1; separate 1.25x / 2x speed comparisons and correctness failures
retain their own criteria. A local pass does not imply an L4 latency pass.

## Measurement history


A cross-repository example that consumes this engine (mesh to signed field, exported as `sdf`/`origin`/`pitch`) lives in the motion engine repository: `examples/compose/field_sdf_to_contacts_to_graph.py`.

## Innovation target 3: free-form section measurement


Before execution: regenerate the frozen topology fixture at its existing 12 iterations,
isotropic resampling and pitch, then measure the unchanged primitive coverage and every
Z-section loop. Instrument gates: positive volume, watertight isosurface, all section
loops closed, coverage strictly between zero and one. Two executions must produce
byte-identical numerical reports and full field arrays. Optimiser timing is redirected
to stderr and excluded from the report. No candidate recipe is designed yet.
The later volume gate remains the existing 0.5*pitch*surface_area/volume bound, computed
from this measured reference before design. Schema and baseline files remain unchanged.

Instrument correction before candidate design: the first two section-probe runs
were byte-identical but fitted untranslated mesh coordinates and measured primitive
coverage 0.715195379920. The shipped baseline translates by the fixture origin minus
one padding pitch before fitting. The probe now reproduces that coordinate convention;
the earlier coverage value is retained here, not treated as the baseline's 67% result.
No geometry, optimiser, fitting tolerance or gate was changed.

Measured mechanism table, before recipe design:

| quantity | measured |
|---|---|
| field shape / pitch | 120 x 120 x 7 / 4.8 |
| primitive coverage / free-form area | 0.670502171861 / 158845.597139 |
| reference volume / surface area | 2802657.825415 / 482083.897292 |
| fixed rasterisation volume bound | 0.412822907959 relative |
| section loop counts, bottom to top | 3, 3, 5, 3, 3; all closed |
| section polygon vertices, total per section | 672, 742, 674, 736, 668 |

The changing loop count prevents a single fixed-topology loft. Candidate design:
represent each section as an outer polygon loft across one pitch, subtract its
nested hole lofts, then union the five slabs. This is a piecewise reconstruction,
not a smooth cross-section correspondence claim. Every operation uses the existing
`sketch_profile`, `loft`, `boolean_cut` and `boolean_union` schema and executor.
Polygon simplification is limited to pitch/8 and must preserve loop topology.

Gates registered before implementation/execution: valid schema and all executor
steps PASS; resulting body valid and one solid; relative volume error strictly
below the fixed measured rasterisation bound above; rebuilt volume equal to the
independent polygon-area slab integral within 1e-8 relative; contour simplification
Hausdorff distance <=pitch/8 and containment hierarchy unchanged. Two full recipe
and numerical-report files must be byte-identical. No threshold may be increased.


Target 3 measured recipe result:

| gate | measured | limit / verdict |
|---|---|---|
| schema and executor | 56 operations, including 17 lofts | all steps PASS |
| final body | valid, 1 solid | PASS |
| reference / rebuilt volume | 2802657.825415 / 2845675.788847 | relative error 0.0153489887501 < 0.412822907959: PASS |
| independent polygon slab integral | 2845675.788847 | relative error 8.1819104118e-16 <1e-8: PASS |
| maximum contour simplification distance | 0.597329126496 | <=0.600000000000: PASS |
| two complete recipe/report executions | byte-identical | PASS |

Evidence under `src/field_engine/artifacts/`: `field_recipe_sections_probe.json`
and `.npy`, `field_to_recipe_loft_v1_recipe.json`, `field_to_recipe_loft_v1.json`.
The recipe SHA256 is `7a4611170260936d5bca7723c6f379fb5791bb90e997d44525819ebb5256e5e6`.
Run the section probe first to regenerate the input; then run
`python src/field_engine/field_to_recipe_loft_v1.py` twice. Install the declared
Shapely dependency along with the existing recipe dependencies.

The target's volume criterion is met on the topology optimiser's synthetic fixture.
All material is expressed through the existing schema, including the regions the
primitive-only baseline leaves free-form. This is a section-slab recipe with ruled
lofts, not a smooth surface reconstruction or a structural/manufacturing certificate.
Topology changes are handled by explicit slab pieces and hole cuts; cross-section
correspondence and smoothness across slab boundaries remain future work. No baseline,
recipe schema, executor or README file was changed.

### Innovation target 4: block-layout mechanism, before adaptive design

Rebuild the frozen synthetic bracket at its existing automatically selected fine
pitch and origin. Apply the unchanged block classifier at block sizes1,2,4,8,16
to the exact same dense signed field. Measure active voxels, metadata-plus-tile
bytes, dense build bytes, sign mismatches and full layout hashes. This isolates
block-size storage overhead from per-block pitch/refinement and is not an adaptive
implementation. No time or peak-memory improvement is claimed.

Fixed instrument gates: two full rebuilds/measurement dictionaries bit-identical,
original fine active voxel count718848 reproduced, every sparse reconstruction's
occupancy exactly equal to the dense sign field. No geometric approximation or
classifier tolerance change. The later adaptive target remains <=0.5 of the frozen
fine active voxels. CPU visibility configured before Warp import, launches on CPU.


Measured block-layout table, two complete CPU runs identical:

| block side | active voxels | ratio to frozen fine | stored bytes incl metadata | sign mismatches |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | 277326 | 0.385792267628 | 4405356 | 0 |
| 2 | 429168 | 0.597021901709 | 2222456 | 0 |
| 4 | 565120 | 0.786146723647 | 2332200 | 0 |
| 8 | 718848 | 1.000000000000 | 2886624 | 0 |
| 16 | 958464 | 1.333333333333 | 3835728 | 0 |

Evidence: `artifacts/field_block_layout_probe.json`. All three instrument gates
PASS. Dense build input=2186748 bytes, shape207x139x19, pitch0.7483641718372458.
Block1 meets the active-voxel target but costs more bytes than block8 because of
metadata; this is a storage negative, not an adaptive solution. Block2 misses
<=0.5 despite lower byte storage. The measured tradeoff motivates hierarchical
inactive blocks and packed leaf metadata, keeping exact near-surface samples.
No baseline or tolerance changed; no timing/peak-memory claim.

### Target 4 adaptive design and gates before execution

The table shows block1's sample benefit and metadata penalty. A separate adaptive
tree therefore begins at block8, subdivides only blocks marked active by the
unchanged classifier through sides4,2,1, and stores exact dense samples at active
unit leaves. Inactive leaves retain the classifier's signed bound, not exact
far-field distance. Breadth-first eight-child allocation permits implicit child
addresses using branch rank. Pack node types into2 bits and store branch/value
rank checkpoints every256 nodes; active values remain float32. Queries read this
packed structure, not a retained dense field or expanded node array.

Fixed gates: two full rebuilds/tree+reconstruction hashes and metric dictionaries
bit-identical; active voxels<=0.5 frozen fine; serialized numeric storage including
metadata/header<=frozen fine's metadata+tiles; zero occupancy mismatches over the
entire dense grid; exact sample bytes wherever abs(dense)<=one fine pitch; finite
queries. No relaxation if a gate fails. This is an adaptive narrow-band storage
experiment; dense construction memory, far-field distance accuracy, continuous
interpolation, build/query speed and engine integration are not certified here.


Measured adaptive-block table, two complete CPU rebuilds:

| metric | run1 | run2 | gate/result |
|---|---|---|---|
| active voxels | 277326 | 277326 | ratio0.385792267628<=0.5 PASS |
| stored bytes incl header/metadata | 1253511 | 1253511 | <=2886624 PASS |
| tree nodes | 512444 | 512444 | diagnostic |
| whole-grid sign mismatches | 0 | 0 | exact PASS |
| narrow-band samples | 126056 | 126056 | all sample bytes exact PASS |
| tree hash | bceae26a8084e443bfe86f394d06cf7ed8e84badb39fdbabe6079ee01fa282ff | same | byte identity PASS |

Evidence: `artifacts/field_adaptive_blocks_v1.json`. All six gates PASS. Inactive
leaf sides4 and2 coexist with side1 near-surface leaves, so this is a block-size
hierarchy rather than a uniformly smaller block grid. The bracket active-voxel
criterion is met with smaller numeric storage as well. This does not certify
peak construction memory, far-field distance fidelity, continuous interpolation,
query/build speed, or integration. No baseline, geometry or tolerance changed.

### RT contract measurement: gates registered before execution

The frozen default computes signed +Z winding occupancy and a half-pitch-corrected
Euclidean distance transform of that mask. Its optional parity and true mesh
point-distance paths are separate contracts. Before RT design, measure five fixed
box soups at pitch0.25: ordinary, offset, globally reversed, overlapping and nested.
Record every full mask, distance array and ray-hit array twice. Fixed observation
gates: exact full-array repeats; exact metric tables; finite distances; unchanged
ordinary-box occupancy/distance after global orientation reversal. Parity mismatch
and analytic-distance errors are measurements, not tolerances to tune. No timing
or RT implementation claim is made by this probe.


Measured table before backend design; evidence:
`artifacts/field_rt_contract_probe.json` and full arrays in the matching NPZ.

| case | ray columns | triangle hits | winding occupied | parity occupied | sign mismatches | EDT versus analytic maximum absolute error |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| box | 441 | 512 | 4096 | 4096 | 0 | 0.3080126941204071 |
| offset | 484 | 512 | 4096 | 4096 | 0 | 0.1538455336083162 |
| reversed | 441 | 512 | 4096 | 4096 | 0 | 0.3080126941204071 |
| overlap | 575 | 1024 | 5672 | 3152 | 2520 | not measured |
| nested | 441 | 640 | 4096 | 3584 | 512 | not measured |

At the fixed interior point of each single box, the +Z hit distance is2 while
the nearest face distance is0.25. Neither that ray distance nor exact triangle
point distance reproduces the default EDT contract. Sample offset is0, jitter
fraction0.0001; the existing code comments describing half-cell centres do not
change these measured inputs. Global orientation reversal leaves the complete
occupancy and distance bytes unchanged. No tolerance has been assigned to the
observed semantic differences; parity is a measured unsuitable replacement for
the default union/winding contract on the overlap/nested fixtures.

Next backend design must preserve signed hit multiplicity and the half-open
z boundary convention, then reuse the frozen mask-distance transform if exact
default-array identity is claimed. True point-to-triangle distance requires a
separate contract and cannot be certified by a vertical closest-hit result.
An RT speedup remains unmeasured and no backend has been promoted.

bound, not an accuracy tolerance. No accepted speed claim before measurement.

### Exact-column build and caller contract

Build against externally installed official OptiX9.0 headers and a compatible CUDA
toolkit; no SDK or compiled binary is vendored. Example from repository root:

```sh
c++ -shared -fPIC -pthread -O2 -std=c++17 -I"$OPTIX_INCLUDE" -I"$CUDA_INCLUDE" src/field_engine/rt_columns_v1/api.cpp -L"$CUDA_LIB" -lcudart -ldl -o columns.so
nvcc -ptx --fmad=false -std=c++17 -I"$OPTIX_INCLUDE" src/field_engine/rt_columns_v1/program.cu -o columns.ptx
nvcc -shared -Xcompiler=-fPIC -O3 src/field_engine/edt_native_large_v1/edt.cu -o edt_large.so
```

Select the target architecture explicitly for deployment; recorded evidence used
compute_89/sm_89. PTX compilation MUST disable FMA to preserve the measured double
operation sequence. Resolve the CUDA runtime through the caller's normal loader.

`mesh_field_columns_v1.PreparedMeshField(vertices, faces, pitch, lo,
winding_library=..., ptx=..., edt_library=..., global_shape=None,
margin_vox_pad=2)` owns an immutable mesh/grid. `vertices` is finite Nx3 and
`faces` is integral Mx3; the public wrapper bounds corners and query coordinates
to absolute1e12, grid axes to512 and cells to1048576. These capacity bounds do not
certify numerical equivalence at every admitted input. Native column storage
admits64 hits per column in this composed adapter; overflow is an error.

Use a context manager; `evaluate()` returns owned `(gmin, shape, surface, solid,
distance)` with bool masks and float32 half-pitch distances. Closed consistently
oriented meshes use custom RT candidates plus CPU composite keys; other meshes use
ordered CPU GWN. Every evaluation recomputes sign/distance, not cached outputs.
Native and wrapper handles are same-thread; close invalidates evaluation, native
failure latches refusal to retry. No implicit input, cache, log or report files
are written by the class. The module's command-line probe explicitly writes only
its documented synthetic reports. Caller owns hardware coordination and library
paths. Original CPU and earlier failed RT entry points remain unchanged.

### Field-recipe regression oracle, 2026-09-13

`python probes/field_engine/field_recipe_oracle_v1.py` builds four independent
polygon occupancy fixtures, passes their implicit fields through the existing
section-loft fitter and recipe executor, then classifies each lattice point
against the resulting B-rep with the solid classifier. Comparisons report
occupancy and the derived half-pitch corrected EDT per feature, plus occupancy
differences per section. Grid shape is 48 x 48 x 5 at pitch 0.2, with a fixed
nonaligned origin. Every fixture is rebuilt twice; full field hashes and recipe
hashes match. The unchanged executor must produce one valid solid at each run.

| Feature | Occupancy differences | EDT differences | Planted error occupancy / EDT differences |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| Square | 0 | 0 | — |
| Chamfered corner | 0 | 0 | 135 / 682, chamfer removed |
| Twelve-sided profile | 0 | 0 | 171 / 4212, replaced by 96-sided circle approximation |
| Through hole | 0 | 0 | — |

Both planted errors repeat exactly and are rejected by the zero-difference
criterion. Three gates pass: zero round-trip difference, complete repeats and
planted-error detection. This certifies sampled occupancy and its grid EDT on
these extruded fixtures, not continuous B-rep equality, arbitrary topopt fields,
scalar-field amplitudes or errors smaller than the grid can resolve. The source
implicit fields use +1/-1 occupancy values; the compared distances are derived
independently from each occupancy using the same frozen EDT convention.
The oracle is part of `pytest tests/test_selftests.py -k field_recipe_regression_oracle`.


### Adaptive lossless blocks: mechanism and preregistration

The failed block sweep changes both storage and the delivered field, including
coarse samples at interfaces. A CPU cost-only pass on the unchanged bracket
fine field (207 x 139 x 19 samples, 125 distinct float32 values) instead splits
the longest varying axis recursively and collapses exactly invariant axes.
At leaf size 512 and reduced-payload cutoff 64, the predicted retained layout
is 757 nodes, 102970 float32 samples and 451244 bytes including 52 bytes per
node. The fixed global-fine comparator stores 718848 active samples / 2875392
sample bytes; its own metadata is not credited to the candidate.

Candidate: immutable adaptive sample blocks, with disjoint integer index
bounds. Collapse an axis only when every float32 bit equals its first slice.
Choose children only when their payload plus metadata costs less than the
parent representation. Query the eight original fine-grid neighbors through
the tree and apply trilinear interpolation once on that common fine grid;
do not interpolate directly between different leaf resolutions or overwrite
fine faces with coarse samples. No change to mesh signing, EDT or the baseline.

Gates before implementation/execution: full-grid reconstruction bit-identical;
all original 4000 bracket surface queries bit-identical to the global-fine
query; fixed-seed interior and boundary queries bit-identical; metadata and
payload bytes at most half the fine active-sample bytes; payload samples at
most half fine active samples; two independent builds/queries bit-identical;
input arrays unchanged; reject unsupported inputs. Include thin-axis, random,
constant and signed-zero controls. Record the original median/P95 accuracy
and explicit two-sided block-face query parity. No tolerance is loosened.
The builder may consume a dense fine reference temporarily: this target is
retained representation size, not peak build memory, faster mesh rasterization,
a coarse PDE solve or AMR flux conservation.

### Adaptive block result, 2026-09-13

The separate `adaptive_sample_blocks_v1.AdaptiveSampleBlocks` stores the
bracket field in 757 adaptive spatial nodes and 102970 float32 samples.
The sample ratio is 0.14324307781339032; array payload, node metadata and
56-byte grid header total 451300 bytes, ratio 0.15695251290954415 against
2875392 reference sample bytes. Both are below the fixed half-size gate.
These are array/header accounting, not Python allocator or process RSS.

Two independently regenerated bracket builds preserve all 546687 grid samples
bit-for-bit, including 4000 original surface queries, 8193 seeded interior/
exterior queries and 756 two-sided split-face queries per build. The delivered
median/P95 remain exactly 0.1379377841949463 / 0.3741820752620697. Node arrays,
payloads, complete grids and all query arrays repeat exactly. Six controls
(random, each thin axis, constant and signed zero) pass full-grid/query checks
in paired builds; 20 invalid inputs are refused. A ninth lifetime gate verifies

`CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES='' pytest tests/test_selftests.py -k adaptive_sample_blocks`

in separate workers and are not a paired RT-versus-CUDA throughput comparison.
The original RT cold-speed failure remains unchanged. This explicit CUDA scan
alternative closes the first-use L gate; it does not establish cold wins on
S/M, other geometries or other devices. The prepared RT backend remains available.

Reproduce with the three explicit library variables `CUDA_COLUMNS_LIBRARY`,
`NATIVE_EDT_LARGE_LIBRARY`, `COLUMN_MASK_LIBRARY`, and `FIELD_PLATE_STL`, then
`python probes/field_engine/field_cuda_columns_cold_probe.py` in an exclusive GPU
window. Build the scan, existing large EDT and mask source as separate CUDA
shared libraries with the target architecture; keep `--fmad=false` for the
scan and mask predicates. No OptiX SDK or PTX is needed for this backend.


### CUDA field cross-device capture preregistration

Run the unchanged ten-gate CUDA scan probe on the local GPU under an exclusive
caller-owned lock and kernel fault guards, preserving the L4 reports. Compare
all named field/hit arrays, dtype, shape and bytes against the L4 capture;
require both local legs, both cloud legs and all seven fixtures. Record actual
hardware/software versions. Background graphics utilization is recorded but
is not an accuracy rejection: no performance number is taken from this local
capture. Unknown compute contexts still reject the window. No tolerance or
backend arithmetic changes are permitted to obtain a cross-device result.

### CUDA field cross-device result, 2026-09-13

The unchanged scan passes all ten source gates on the local NVIDIA GeForce
RTX 5070, driver 580.178.04, as on Modal NVIDIA L4, driver 580.95.05. The local
window acquired the shared hardware lock and passed kernel fault guards;
background graphics utilization was 10%, memory 1428 MiB and no competing
compute process was reported. No local performance measurement is claimed.

All 86 named field/hit arrays (9446360 bytes per capture) match across the
two devices in dtype, shape and bytes. Each of the seven fixtures has two
matching legs on each device. The standalone saved-capture audit passes 5/5:
all ten source gates, complete inventories and hashes, within-device repeats,
cross-device equality, and rejection of missing-array/corrupt-hash controls.
The CPU-only pytest wrapper passes (1 test) and needs no GPU to re-audit the
committed evidence. New hardware capture still requires the explicit native
libraries and exclusive hardware window.

Both environments used NumPy 2.5.3, SciPy 1.18.1, trimesh 5.1.0 and libigl 2.6.3
(local installed versions, cloud image build log). Local Python is 3.13.11;
the Modal image uses Python 3.12. Native code is unchanged and compiled for

agreement across these captured environments, not a universal guarantee for
all compilers, hardware or meshes. The L4 timing evidence remains separate.


### Paired local backend timing preregistration

Compare CPU, CUDA scan and uninstrumented RT on original S/M/L plate pitches
2/1/0.5. Each backend/pitch gets a fresh process in each of two reversed-order
legs (12 children total). First native one-shot includes constructor/evaluate/
close with no explicit warm-up. Then record a warm one-shot, a separate prepared
setup/close, and four alternating-order CPU/prepared query pairs. Record all
complete output hashes and arrays. OS/driver/compiler caches are not cleared,
so fresh process is not a claim of a cold system cache.

The caller holds the shared GPU lock; initial utilization must be zero with no
compute context. Kernel fault guards run before and after every child. Capture
CPU tick counters and load averages; other CPU jobs can affect these wall times.
Acceptance is complete 12-child coverage, exact CPU/backend full fields, exact
within/across-leg outputs and the initial idle guard. Performance observations
are retained even when a backend loses; overlapping timing ranges are not a
defensible winner or a statistical throughput claim. CPU background load is
not inferred to be absent merely because the GPU is idle.

The initial zero-utilization attempt captured zero numerical samples: the
reported GPU utilization was 15% with no compute context. A six-sample probe
measured 8/11/14/25/5/5% background activity, attributable to identified graphics
processes. The explicit `--allow-desktop-background` option follows the shared
desktop-aware coordination rule: require no competing compute context and
positively identify every reported graphics executable. Unknown processes
still refuse execution. The initial zero-utilization condition remains a
separate reported boolean; it is not silently relabeled as idle. Numerical
acceptance is unchanged. Timing under this mode describes a running desktop
and does not certify an isolated GPU or absent CPU contention. This guard
change follows a zero-sample refusal, not a discarded unfavorable timing.

### Local backend timing result, 2026-09-13

NVIDIA GeForce RTX 5070, driver 580.178.04, desktop-background mode; initial GPU utilization 15%, memory 1179 MiB. No competing compute context; identified graphics only. All 12 children completed under one lock and kernel fault guards, with 5/5 observer gates and complete field bytes equal across CPU/CUDA/RT and both orders. The zero-utilization condition was false and remains reported as such. No isolated-device or statistical significance claim.

Values below are observed ranges across the two fresh-process orders. Prepared values are the per-child medians of four evaluations; setup and close are separate in the full report.

CPU tick counters and load averages are retained per child.

For these fixtures and conditions, CPU wins fresh S/M calls; the CUDA scan
wins fresh L. A warm CUDA one-shot wins over a warm RT one-shot at all three
sizes. Prepared RT has the lower per-evaluation medians, with substantially

justify a universal automatic backend heuristic. The cold system/compiler
cache is uncontrolled, especially in the first RT process. Full raw times,
132 complete output hash sets and all corresponding arrays are retained.

### Warm RT service preregistration (queue item 20)

One spawned process owns one prepared mesh, replaced explicitly; every query

query and four warm queries each. First latency includes spawn/import/prepare

### Block-field CSG preregistration (queue item 21)

Union/difference operate by min/max on negative-inside samples gathered directly

peak-memory reduction claim. Test two complete bracket/plate/rectangular-hole
chains against independent dense arithmetic and exact B-rep booleans. Require

Bound volume by cells whose analytic 1-Lipschitz field at the cell center is
within half the cell diagonal of zero; require both volumes inside that envelope
and their difference below its width. Report field CSG and B-rep construction
plus boolean separately, with input packing and mesh extraction costs explicit.

### Block-field CSG result

VERIFIED-FRESH: 5/5 gates on the synthetic axis-aligned bracket/plate with two
rectangular through holes. Complete samples, offset samples, packed nodes and
payload, extracted vertices and faces repeat bitwise. Mesh is watertight and
B-rep valid. Mesh volume 967.25 vs exact B-rep 960.0000000000001, absolute error
7.25 (0.7552%). Conservative cell envelope [507, 1513], width 1006, contains
both volumes. This deliberately conservative bound is wider than the part's
volume and does not establish a tight general accuracy guarantee.

Field CSG takes 243.871–243.896 ms, versus 29.091–32.110 ms for B-rep primitive
construction plus booleans. Input field packing adds 40.358 ms; mesh extraction
adds 2.325–3.414 ms. Field CSG loses this timing comparison even before input
packing. This is two local CPU observations under ordinary concurrent desktop
conditions, not an isolated performance benchmark. Output retains 136020 host
array bytes. No peak-memory or arbitrary curved-part claim. Offset is checked
against explicit float64 subtraction rounded once to float32; min/max results
are level sets and need not remain Euclidean signed distances. Source operands
are gathered from blocks, with a transient dense output used for packing.
Evidence: `reports/field_csg.json`, `reports/field_csg_arrays.npz`.


### Fused block CSG preregistration

Preserve the eager operation order and every intermediate float32 rounding;
gather each input once per chunk, retain intermediate chunk arrays until their
last use, and pack only the final result. Require full sample and packed-array
equality against the unchanged eager chain in two reversed-order CPU runs,
exact equality with the committed bracket/plate/hole capture, five paired
arithmetic controls (signed zero, shared operands, offsets, cancellation and
chunk boundaries), nine invalid-input controls including intermediate overflow.
Require at least 1.25x eager/fused speedup in both observations. Input packing
and mesh extraction are excluded from both times. No isolated CPU timing claim.

### Tiled block CSG preregistration

Keep the fused variant's instruction order and rounding. Replace per-point
leaf routing with intersections of rectangular leaf boxes and broadcast of
invariant axes; tiles hold at most 8192 samples. Require exact packed outputs
and saved fixture equality in two reversed-order comparisons, 25 tile/arithmetic
controls on thin axes, signed zeros and non-multiple dimensions, nine invalid
controls, and at least 1.25x over point-gather fusion in both observations.
Keep the dense output and single final packing; do not claim lower peak memory.

### Fused and tiled CSG results

Both separate variants are VERIFIED-FRESH, 6/6 own gates each. Frozen eager
implementation and original CSG reports are unchanged. Every output sample,
node, payload and origin matches the original bracket/plate/hole field exactly;
this connects the variants to its existing mesh/volume evidence without
inventing a new or tighter rasterisation bound.

| Comparison | Leg 0 ms | Leg 1 ms | Paired speedup |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| Eager chain, four packs | 245.605 | 242.442 | reference |
| Fused point-gather, one pack | 132.039 | 132.077 | 1.860x / 1.836x |
| Point-gather in tiled comparison | 134.528 | 134.323 | reference |
| Direct leaf tiles, one pack | 27.681 | 27.552 | 4.860x / 4.875x |

The first improvement eliminates intermediate packing and repeated input reads.
The second replaces per-point tree routing with leaf-box intersections and
broadcasts invariant axes. Both preserve operation order and each intermediate
float32 rounding; even an overflowing intermediate later hidden by min/max is
rejected. Five paired fused arithmetic controls and 25 tiled controls pass,
including signed zero, offset cancellation, shared operands, thin axes and
non-multiple tile dimensions. Nine invalid-input controls pass per variant.

Times are two reversed-order local CPU observations per comparison under
ordinary concurrent desktop load. Input packing and surface extraction are
excluded equally. The earlier B-rep numbers were measured separately; these
observations do not establish a paired B-rep speed win. Operands stay packed,
intermediate arrays are chunk/tile-sized, and a dense final output remains
transient for packing. No general complexity, peak-memory or GPU claim.
Raw reports and full sample captures are `reports/field_csg_fused.json`,
`reports/field_csg_fused_arrays.npz`, `reports/field_csg_tiled.json` and
`reports/field_csg_tiled_arrays.npz`.

The program API takes packed fields plus ordered three-item instructions:
`('union', left_id, right_id)`, `('difference', left_id, right_id)` or
`('offset', left_id, distance)`. Input ids start at zero; instruction i produces
id `len(fields) + i`. References must name inputs or earlier instructions. At
most 256 inputs and 1024 instructions are accepted. Every input must share the
same origin, pitch and shape. The final instruction is returned as packed
samples. For five bracket/plate/hole inputs, the measured program is:

```python
program = [('union', 0, 1), ('union', 5, 2),
           ('union', 3, 4), ('difference', 6, 7)]
result = evaluate(fields, program)
```

Require exact dense arithmetic, repeated full field/packed/mesh arrays,
watertight extracted meshes and valid B-reps. B-rep volume must match analytic
box or sphere-cap/cylinder integration within 1e-8 absolute units cubed. Require
mesh relative volume error at most 1% on every case, both volumes in the original
conservative cell envelope, and strictly reduced volume error on refinement.
The missing-hole negative must change occupancy. Timings are observations,
not a pass/fail speed claim; field and B-rep outputs differ in representation.
Field extraction includes unpacking, while B-rep tessellation is excluded.

### Curved CSG and paired B-rep result

VERIFIED-FRESH: 9/9 gates over eight builds (two fixtures, two pitches, two

bitwise. All extracted meshes are watertight and B-reps valid. Analytic volume

The curved volume reference is independent analytic integration: sphere volume

### Reproduce the CPU CSG slice

`make verify-csg PYTHON=python3` runs seven CSG probes in an isolated temporary
tree with CPU thread limits. Install the repository requirements and a C++17
compiler first. This covers the CPU CSG slice only; it is not queue item 27's
all-module, six-repository `make verify` completion. Existing archived reports
and captures are read-only references. The runner writes one aggregate receipt
at `reports/csg_verify.json`; per-probe temporary outputs are discarded after
comparison. Full arrays, native source hashes and the documented values below
must match exactly. Live performance gates must also pass, so concurrent CPU
load can cause a failure; elapsed times are not exact numeric targets.

The machine-readable contract is the source of truth for gate counts and the
selected volume numbers. A changed number or changed array must be rejected.

<!-- csg-verify-contract:v1 -->
```json
{
  "version": 1,
  "probes": [
    {
      "module": "field_csg_probe.py",
      "report": "field_csg",
      "gate_count": 5,
      "decisive": [
        {
```

CPU CSG verification result: VERIFIED-FRESH, 6/6 aggregate gates. Seven fresh
probes complete in 27.992 s combined child time; 40178560 bytes of complete
arrays compare exactly to archived captures. All documented gate counts and
selected volume numbers match, native source hashes match, live speed gates
pass, and changed-number/changed-array controls are rejected. This is a local
CPU subset receipt, not a Modal or complete-repository claim.


### Integer-envelope EDT preregistration

Separate CUDA kernel beside the exhaustive axis scan. Compute the lower
parabola envelope using exact integer transition positions (strict improvement,
floor division for negative numerators); preserve squared integer distances,
host allocation/transfer contract and host signed-distance arithmetic. Compare
both mask polarities on nine cases: length 512 along each axis, random/noncubic,
single source/target, smallest mixed mask and plate L. Require full exact SciPy
squared values and frozen native outputs, exact signed fields at three pitches,
two reversed orders, four invalid controls and >=2x plate-L pair speedup. Modal
only; no local GPU reservation. Initialization is charged where it occurs.

### Persistent paired EDT preregistration

Retain the failed stateless envelope experiment. A separate thread-owned handle
allocates once, uploads one boolean mask, evaluates both polarities together and
produces signed float32 distance on GPU with explicit round-to-nearest double
sqrt/multiply, float conversion/subtraction and half-pitch correction. Require
all squared values exact to SciPy and all signed values exact to both frozen
native host arithmetic and independent CPU reconstruction at five pitches,
including 1e-12/1e12. Nine cases twice, changed-mask/thread/close controls, setup
and first-query costs separate. Require >=2x plate-L warm signed-field speedup.
This is not a full mesh-stage or 2 ms service claim. Modal only.

### Full mesh service with persistent EDT preregistration

Keep the previous service and mesh-stage implementation frozen. A separate stage
subclass replaces only its EDT seam with a thread-owned persistent pair; RT
columns, open-mesh sign policy, masks, complete output tuple and IPC are unchanged.
Run the original six fresh-worker S/M/L service gate with 30 full responses,
replacement/failure/close controls and the unchanged <=2 ms requirement for every
L warm response. First response includes spawn/import/preparation; warm response
still returns full arrays and recomputes each field. No output cache.

### Persistent mesh component-profile preregistration

The full service still has an unchanged 2 ms gate. Profile its prepared stage
separately: one untimed warmup, two reversed size orders and four timed calls
per size. Wrap already-synchronous RT query, mask and EDT methods without extra
GPU fences. Require 24 complete CPU-equal results, exact repeats and component
times summing to no more than total. This is attribution, not service acceptance.

### EDT and persistent service results (Modal L4)

NVIDIA L4, driver 580.95.05, initial utilization 0% and no compute contexts for

all 18 paired squared fields and signed fields match SciPy/frozen native exactly,
with four invalid controls passing, but plate-L pair costs 4.840 / 5.031 ms vs
exhaustive 5.890 / 9.206 ms, below the required 2x improvement. Long thin cases
can be slower because each line is processed serially; no automatic dispatch.

Persistent paired EDT is VERIFIED-FRESH, 5/5. Warm plate-L signed field costs
1.515 / 1.415 ms vs 9.909 / 9.197 ms for the old stateless/host-arithmetic path.
Both polarities and five pitches, including 1e-12 and 1e12, agree exactly with
SciPy integer distances and independently reconstructed CPU signed arithmetic.
Ninety signed arrays repeat exactly; 54 changed-mask/thread/close controls pass.
First global runtime setup is 196.604 ms on the smallest case. Plate-L setup
is 18.511 / 0.913 ms, with first queries 1.618 / 1.386 ms. Setup is not hidden in
warm timing. Length-512 thin cases still lose to the exhaustive path; the gate
and speed claim are plate-L scoped. Compute Sanitizer memcheck reports zero

instrumented timings are excluded (`reports/edt_persistent_memcheck.json`).

### Device-packed RT readback preregistration

Keep frozen geometry, tracing and compact host ABI. Allocate persistent scratch
at construction, compact strided device hit rows with one kernel and transfer
contiguous depth/sign arrays. Require exact CPU and old compact hits at S/M/L,
24 paired warm calls in alternating backend/size orders, exact repeats, full
64-hit capacity, overflow latch, zero hits, malformed queries and lifecycle
controls (28). Require >=2x L query speedup in all eight observations. Setup
and one warmup are separate; no full field/service timing claim. Modal only.

RT packed-readback first attempt retained as `field_rt_packed_raw_dtype_attempt`:
3/5 gates, with both the 2x timing requirement and a raw dtype comparison failing.
CPU orientations are int8 and the frozen native ABI is int32; the corrected
oracle converts the CPU values losslessly to the native column/depth/sign dtypes
before exact comparison. No tolerance or native arithmetic changes. A separate
nested native-call timer will distinguish C ABI time from Python query overhead;
its time is included in RT query time and must not be double-counted.

### Row-local ordering preregistration

The nested profile isolates substantial Python cost inside the RT wrapper.
Sort each already-grouped hit row by depth/sign with invalid slots last, yielding
the same global column/depth/sign ordering. A separate mask wrapper verifies
column/depth monotonicity instead of sorting again. Keep the original RT library,
GPU mask kernel and persistent EDT identical on both paths. Require full CPU
and previous-stage equality on seven closed/rotated/translated/open/reversed
fixtures, 14 evaluations in reversed timing orders, exact repeats, 17 row-order
and malformed-order controls, and >=1.5x plate-L stage speedup in both runs.

### Two-hit ordering preregistration

The general rowwise variant is retained with its own >=1.5x gate, even if it
misses. Observed plate rows contain zero or two hits; specialize widths <=2
with a vectorized depth/sign compare-swap, preserving signed-zero ties and
stable equal keys. Wider rows use the unchanged general rowwise fallback.
Require all seven full CPU fixtures exact and repeated, all 17 sorting/invalid
controls, and >=1.25x plate-L stage speedup over the general rowwise variant in
both observations. GPU kernels, mask and persistent EDT stay identical.

### Native RT phase-clock preregistration

The query-call observer leaves native time unattributed. Add observer-only host
clocks around ray preparation, uploads, existing trace/synchronize, count readback
and strided hit readback in a separate copy of the compact-query extension.
No extra GPU fence or trace arithmetic change. Require the same 24 complete CPU
equalities/repeats and stage accounting; native phases are nested inside RT
query time, not additional pipeline stages. No performance acceptance claim.

### RT ordering and readback results

Device-packed readback remains OWN-GATE-FAIL, 4/5 after the lossless CPU ABI

correct that oracle. The packed-readback proposal is not adopted by the ordering
variants; they use the original compact RT library.

The nested-call observer passes 4/4 with 24 complete CPU-equal fields. In its L

General rowwise ordering is OWN-GATE-FAIL, 4/5: 14 complete fields are CPU-exact,

Bind immutable mesh/grid at preparation and retrace on every evaluation. Keep

path is fused; open-mesh GWN fallback stays frozen. Require all seven fixture
fields exact to CPU and the two-hit reference, 14 reversed-order evaluations,
exact repeats, 64-hit capacity and overflow latch plus thread/close controls
(20), and >=2x L stage speedup over the two-hit path. Bound shape/hit capacities
unchanged. Preparation includes uploading the fixed query grid; no 2 ms service
claim. GPU execution stays on Modal.

against fixed-512 in both observations; retain the paired-EDT proof's original

### Borrowed shared-memory service preregistration

Add a separate spawned service whose worker recomputes into complete shared

NumPy views. Close stops the worker before unlinking. CPU fake-stage proof must
pass every ownership, recomputation, replacement, failure and mapping control
before GPU benchmarking. Closed-mesh pinning is prepared once; open fallback
remains supported without pinning. Full-field CPU equality and repeat controls
remain mandatory; the end-to-end L query target stays <=2 ms in every measured
order. Borrowed delivery is a different ownership API; owned-copy cost must be
reported separately. Preparation includes attachment and registration, with no
result caching. GPU correctness/timing remain unverified until measured.

The same shared-service probe will also run on the user-authorized local RTX
5070 under the shared GPU lock, with bounded process-group lifetime and kernel
fault guards. Keep all seven fixtures, fourteen workers, complete output
captures and the unchanged <=2 ms gate. Preserve L4 results separately; compare
all captured arrays across devices exactly. Desktop background is recorded and
no cross-machine causal speed claim is made. Compilation precedes the window.

### Conservative RT interval prefilter preregistration

Before the frozen double triangle tests, bound their barycentric numerators
with outward-rounded float intervals. Reject only when one numerator is proven
strictly positive and another strictly negative; this is outside for either
triangle orientation. Uncertain signs always execute the unchanged double
path, including area threshold and depth arithmetic. Compile without FMA
contraction or flush-to-zero. Float endpoint products fit double precision;
monotonic rounded arithmetic makes the intervals enclose the frozen operation
sequence, including subnormal and boundary cases.

CPU proof first: 120,016 fixed-seed random, exact-vertex, edge-adjacent,
large-translation, subnormal and signed-zero cases. Require finite enclosures,
no false rejection, >=25% rejected (nonvacuous) and some uncertain outside cases
retained, plus exact repeats. Then require CUDA helper equality to every CPU
interval/rejection and frozen edge, all original RT hit/capacity controls, and
full owned-service CPU/repeat/lifecycle gates with the unchanged <=2 ms L gate.
No local execution until CUDA proof and complete fields pass on Modal.

The initial CUDA helper comparison fails 3/4 solely on 9,985 lower-bound zero
sign bytes: all numeric values, upper bounds, rejections and frozen double
edges match. RT/service execution was withheld. The CPU emulation omitted
the negative-zero result of exact round-down cancellation. Correct the CPU
operation and add an explicit five-case signed-zero control, retaining both
initial archives and reports. This follows the specified
[CUDA 12.9 subtraction behavior](https://docs.nvidia.com/cuda/archive/12.9.1/pdf/CUDA_Math_API.pdf).
Native arithmetic and bit-exact gates are unchanged; no zero normalization or
tolerance is introduced.

### Strict-sign interval multiplication preregistration

Add a separate prefilter variant that selects the two extremal products when
both input intervals have strict known signs. Intervals touching/crossing zero
retain the general four-product path. Require every bound, rejection and frozen
double numerator bit-identical to the independent CPU proof, plus the same raw
RT and full owned-service controls and <=2 ms L gate. Record input hashes and
full input arrays in the new CUDA proof so cross-device numerical comparisons
can first establish identical fixtures. Keep the earlier single L latency
outlier and the initial cross-device fixture audit as failures, without
discarding observations or changing accuracy thresholds.

The earlier combined memory/cross-device receipt remains OWN-GATE-FAIL:
all three memchecks are clean and full hit/field archives match, but 9,148
double helper numerators differ across devices. A CPU-only fixture audit on
a Modal GPU host identifies different inputs: NumPy 2.5.3 on that host produces
`0x1.4f8b588e368f0p-17` for vectorized `10**-5`, while the local host produces
`0x1.4f8b588e368f1p-17`. All other scales match. Returned remote inputs differ
in 31,949 triangle and 6,692 query coordinates and reproduce every remote GPU
numerator exactly on the local CPU. A generic cloud CPU host instead matched
the local inputs, so version labels alone do not establish fixture identity.

Keep the original generator and captures. The new strict-sign variant uses a
separate portable fixture generator with explicit hexadecimal binary64 scales
and the same seed/case layout. Require full input hashes and arrays to match
before comparing GPU outputs; this changes test-data generation, not geometry
arithmetic or any tolerance. Full service fixtures never used this generator
and already match exactly across devices.

Corrected CPU proof passes 8/8. The CUDA helper passes 4/4 against each host's

The RT hit proof passes 4/4 per device with 24 complete hit sets, exact repeats

Full owned service remains OWN-GATE-FAIL, 4/5 on both devices. All 70 fields and

and its cause is not isolated. Copies are inside the service call. Local

lock window ended with clean journal guards; no settings changed.

All three Modal memchecks report zero errors and reproduce their own complete
archives exactly. Raw hits and full fields also match across devices. The

All three memchecks report zero errors. Complete archives including portable
inputs match across devices and instrumented runs: 437 arrays, 67,316,098 bytes.
The instrumented owned-service latency failure remains explicit. The earlier
unmatched-fixture cross-device receipt stays failed; this new receipt does not
rewrite it. Evidence: `reports/field_rt_interval_portable.json`,
`reports/field_rt_interval_sign_gpu.json`, `reports/field_rt_interval_sign_hits.json`,
`reports/field_interval_sign_service.json`, their local/full-array counterparts,
`reports/interval_sign_local_window.json` and
`reports/interval_sign_memory_cross_device.json`.


### Exact EDT quotient preregistration

Replace variable integer division in a separate paired-EDT envelope variant.
Use the exact constant-two floor for adjacent sites; otherwise obtain a float
quotient estimate and correct it with integer inequalities until the exact
floor is reached. The estimate is never the accepted result without correction.
EDT bounds keep numerator magnitude below 2^20 and denominator in [2,1022];
64-bit products protect correction comparisons. Signed output rounding and
parabola ordering/ties remain frozen.

CPU proof first: all 14,680,071 numerators across seven dense denominator sets,
plus quotient-boundary cases covering all 511 EDT denominators. Require exact
integer floors and repeats, with no tolerance. Then require CUDA equality for
all arrays, the nine-mask/five-pitch signed and squared field proof and lifecycle
controls, paired >=1.25x warm plate-L EDT improvement against the persistent
integer-division reference, and the unchanged <=2 ms full owned-service gate.
The service will use the frozen general RT interval prefilter; previous strict-
sign and general prefilter timing failures remain separate. Modal numerical
proof precedes any bounded local execution.

### Prepared local RT interval preregistration

Move coordinate-bound preparation out of individual triangle predicates. Bound
geometry and query coordinates relative to the existing anchor with one outward
float neighbor on each side of the rounded coordinate. Their interval difference
encloses the frozen world-space double subtraction; uncertain signs still fall
through to the unchanged area, barycentric and depth operations. Rejecting
candidates need only prepared float bounds. Raw changing-query API refreshes
query bounds every call; the immutable full-field grid prepares them once.
No trace hits, masks or field outputs are cached.

Require CPU enclosure/no-false-rejection and >=25% nonvacuous rejection on all
120016 portable cases with both local and zero anchors, exact repeats and
uncertain outside cases retained. Then require native preparation/bounds and
full CUDA outputs exact to CPU, all raw hit/capacity/changed-query controls and
70 full owned-service CPU fields with the unchanged <=2 ms L gate. New parameter
layout and buffer lifetime require memory validation. Keep all quotient and
prior prefilter negatives; do not infer performance from preprocessing alone.

### Exact quotient results

CPU floor proof passes 5/5: 14,680,071 dense cases and 11,557 boundary cases.
CUDA proof passes 4/4 with complete repeated floor arrays and six invalid
controls. Paired EDT remains OWN-GATE-FAIL, 4/5: all 180 complete signed/squared
arrays and 54 controls pass, but candidate warm L 1.408 / 1.481 ms against
reference 1.481 / 1.404 ms misses the registered 1.25x improvement.

Full owned service also remains 4/5 on Modal L4, with 70 exact full fields and
59 controls. Warm L is 3.035–3.576 ms, above the unchanged 2 ms limit. The
quotient variant is not adopted and was not run locally. Three memchecks report
zero errors and all 553 arrays (295,300,162 bytes) match the uninstrumented
archives exactly. Both instrumented performance failures remain explicit.
Evidence: the four `field_edt_quotient_cpu`, `field_edt_quotient_gpu`,
`field_quotient_pair`, `field_quotient_service` reports and captures, and
`reports/quotient_memcheck.json`. Numerical exactness does not establish a speed
benefit; the original service goal remains open.


The initial prepared-helper CUDA comparison fails 3/4: native geometry/query
preparation, rejection decisions and frozen double edges match, but interval
endpoint bytes differ. The CPU reference rounded float endpoint subtraction
through one double value; that loses a tiny residual when subtracting, for
example, a float subnormal from 1. Correct this new CPU reference with an
error-free double sum/residual before directed float rounding, and add explicit
small-term/cancellation controls. Preserve both initial archives and reports.
Native code, strict bit equality and geometric tolerances remain unchanged.

### Prepared local interval results

The original full owned-service target passes locally, 5/5 on RTX 5070:
all eight warm L calls take 1.232007–1.356554 ms, including independent result
copies. Setup takes 613.279 / 617.524 ms; first queries take 1.493 / 1.542 ms.
These are separate costs. Warm M includes a retained 1.873464 ms observation.
Modal L4 remains OWN-GATE-FAIL, 4/5, with warm L 2.842467–3.715790 ms.
The local result does not establish a device-independent latency guarantee.

Each device checks 70 complete CPU-exact owned results and 59 lifecycle/capacity
controls. Raw RT passes 4/4 with 24 paired hit sets and 30 controls, including
changed query coordinates and refusal of both mismatched PTX/native layouts.
CPU preparation proof passes 8/8 on 120016 portable cases: 65738 proven rejects,
24512 uncertain outside cases retained. The corrected native helper passes 4/4
on each device with exact prepared bounds, predicates and frozen double edges.
All 442 arrays (85,558,530 bytes), including complete inputs, match across devices.
Three memchecks report zero errors with every complete array exact; the
instrumented service latency failure remains recorded. Initial CPU/helper
reference failures remain in the initial reports and full captures.

The bounded local window lasted 18.67 seconds; kernel-journal guards passed
before, between and after probes. No compute process was present at entry;
desktop activity was recorded. The GPU lock was released. Evidence is in the
`field_rt_prepared_cpu`, `field_rt_prepared_gpu`, `field_rt_prepared_hits` and
`field_prepared_service` reports/captures (local suffix for RTX), plus
`prepared_cross_device.json`, `prepared_memcheck.json`, and
`prepared_local_window.json` under `reports/`.

Prepared bounds are uploaded once for immutable geometry/grid coordinates;
every field query still retraces all geometry and recomputes mask, surface and
EDT. CUDA graph capture covers only the tail. Frozen exact predicates and
integer-division EDT remain in use; prior quotient and prefilter speed failures
are retained.
